Okay, let me try to figure out this problem step by step. So, there are 150 workers initially hired to finish a job in a certain number of days. But every day after the first, workers start dropping out. Specifically, 4 workers drop out on the second day, another 4 on the third day, and so on. Because of this, it took 8 more days than originally planned to complete the work. We need to find out how many days were originally supposed to be taken to finish the job.

First, let me denote the original number of days as 'n'. So, without any dropouts, the total work would have been 150 workers multiplied by n days, which is 150n worker-days.

But since workers are dropping out each day starting from day 2, the actual number of workers each day forms an arithmetic sequence. On day 1, there are 150 workers. On day 2, it's 150 - 4 = 146 workers because 4 dropped out. Then on day 3, another 4 drop out, making it 142, and so on.

This sequence decreases by 4 each day. The number of days the work actually took is n + 8 days because it took 8 more days than originally planned.

So, we can model this with an arithmetic series where the first term a₁ = 150, common difference d = -4, and the number of terms k = n + 8. The total work done in these actual days is the sum of this series.

The formula for the sum S of the first k terms of an arithmetic series is:
S = (k/2) * [2a₁ + (k - 1)d]

Substituting the known values:
S = ((n + 8)/2) * [2*150 + (n + 8 - 1)*(-4)]

This sum S should be equal to the total work, which is also equal to 150n worker-days. So we set up the equation:

((n + 8)/2) * [300 + (n + 7)*(-4)] = 150n

Let me simplify this step by step.

First, let's compute the term inside the brackets:
[300 - 4(n + 7)] = 300 - 4n - 28 = 272 - 4n

Now, plug that back into the equation:

((n + 8)/2) * (272 - 4n) = 150n

Multiply both sides by 2 to eliminate the denominator:

(n + 8)(272 - 4n) = 300n

Now, expand the left side:
n*(272 - 4n) + 8*(272 - 4n) = 300n
272n - 4n² + 2176 - 32n = 300n

Combine like terms:
(272n - 32n) - 4n² + 2176 = 300n
240n - 4n² + 2176 = 300n

Bring all terms to one side:

-4n² + 240n + 2176 - 300n = 0
-4n² - 60n + 2176 = 0

Multiply both sides by -1 to make the coefficient of n² positive:

4n² + 60n - 2176 = 0

Divide all terms by 4 to simplify:

n² + 15n - 544 = 0

Now, we can solve this quadratic equation using the quadratic formula:
n = [-b ± sqrt(b² - 4ac)]/(2a)
Here a=1, b=15, c=-544.

So,
Discriminant D = 15² - 4*1*(-544) = 225 + 2176 = 2401

Square root of D is sqrt(2401)=49

Thus,
n = [-15 ± 49]/2

We discard the negative solution because days can't be negative:
n = (-15 + 49)/2 = (34)/2 = 17

So, n=17. That means the original number of days planned was 17 days.

First term a₁=150
Last term a_25 = 150 + (25-1)*(-4)=150 - 96=54

So, Sum=(25/2)*(150+54)=(25/2)*204=25*102=2550 worker-days.

Yes, that matches the total work. So it's correct.
</think>

To determine if 330 is part of the sequence starting at 5 and increasing by 6 each time (5, 11, 17, 23, ...), I first identify that this is an arithmetic sequence.

Here, a_1 is 5 and d is 6. Plugging these into the formula gives:

a_n = 5 + (n - 1) * 6

Simplifying this equation to solve for n when a_n equals 330:

5 + 6(n - 1) = 330  
6(n - 1) = 325

Since n must be an integer, and 54.1667 is not a whole number, it indicates that 330 does not appear in the sequence.

To determine whether **330** is a term of the arithmetic sequence:  
*Sequence:* 5, 11, 17, 23, ...  

### Step-by-Step Solution:

   - **First Term (\(a_1\))**: 5
   - **Common Difference (\(d\))**:  
     \( d = 11 - 5 = 6 \)

2. **Use the Arithmetic Sequence Formula:**
   
   The general formula for the \(n^{th}\) term of an arithmetic sequence is:
   \[
   a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d
   \]
   
3. **Set Up the Equation to Find \(n\):**
   
   We want to find if there exists an integer \(n\) such that:
   \[
   a_n = 330
   \]
   
   Plugging in the known values:
   \[
   5 + (n - 1) \times 6 = 330
   \]

First, I need to calculate the simple interest earned each year on an initial sum of Rs. 2000 at a rate of 16% per annum.

Simple Interest (SI) is calculated using the formula:

SI = Principal × Rate × Time

Here, the principal amount is Rs. 2000, the annual interest rate is 16%, and the time period for each year is 1 year.

Calculating the SI for one year:
SI = 2000 × 0.16 × 1
= 320

Each subsequent year, since simple interest remains constant, the interest earned will also be Rs. 320.

Now, examining whether these interests form an Arithmetic Progression (AP), I observe that each term in the sequence of interests is the same (Rs. 320). In an AP, consecutive terms have a common difference. Here, the difference between any two consecutive terms is zero because all interests are equal.

Therefore, the interests form an AP with a common difference of 0.

To find the total interest earned over 30 years using this fact:
Total Interest = Number of Terms × Common Difference + (Number of Terms × (Number of Terms - 1)) / 2
= 30 × 320 + (30 × 29) / 2 × 0
Since the common difference is zero, the second term becomes zero.

Calculating further:
Total Interest = 30 × 320
= 9600

So, the total interest earned over 30 years is Rs. 9600.
</think>

To solve the problem, let's break it down step by step.

**Given:**
- Principal (\( P \)) = Rs. 2000
- Rate of Interest (\( R \)) = 16% per annum
- Time Period = Each year for 30 years

**Simple Interest Formula:**
\[
\text{Simple Interest (SI)} = P \times R \times T
\]
Where:
- \( SI \) is the simple interest,
- \( P \) is the principal amount,
- \( R \) is the rate of interest per period,
- \( T \) is the time in years.

**Calculating Annual Interest:**
For each year, since the interest is calculated on the original principal:
\[
SI = 2000 \times \frac{16}{100} \times 1 = Rs.\ 320
\]

**Checking for Arithmetic Progression (AP):**
In an AP, consecutive terms have a constant difference. Here, each year's interest is the same:
\[
SI_{\text{each year}} = Rs.\ 320
\]
Thus, the interests form an **Arithmetic Progression** with a common difference of \( 0 \).

**Total Interest Over 30 Years:**
Since all interests are equal to Rs. 320:
\[
\text{Total Interest} = SI \times \text{Number of Years} = 320 \times 30 = 9600
\]

- Common difference (d) = -2
- Last term (aₙ) = 4

4 = 46 + (n - 1)(-2)

Simplifying the equation:
(n - 1)(-2) = 4 - 46

To determine the number of rows in the flower bed, we can model this scenario using an **arithmetic progression (AP)** since each row has a constant difference in the number of rose plants.

**Given:**
- *First term* (\(a_1\)) = 46
- *Common difference* (\(d\)) = -2 (since each row decreases by 2 rose plants)
- *Last term* (\(a_n\)) = 4

We need to find the number of rows, which corresponds to finding \(n\) in the AP.

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Understand the Formula for the nth Term of an AP:**
   
   The general formula for the \(n^{th}\) term of an arithmetic progression is:
   \[
   a_n = a_1 + (n - 1)d
   \]
   
2. **Plug in the Known Values:**
   
   Substitute \(a_1 = 46\), \(d = -2\), and \(a_n = 4\) into the formula:
   \[
   4 = 46 + (n - 1)(-2)
   \]
   
3. **Solve for \(n\):**
   
   \[
   4 = 46 - 2(n - 1)
   \]
   
   Subtract 46 from both sides:
   \[
   4 - 46 = -2(n - 1)
   \]
   \[
   -42 = -2(n - 1)
   \]
   
   Divide both sides by -2:
   \[
   \frac{-42}{-2} = n - 1
   \]
   \[
   21 = n - 1
   \]
   
   Add 1 to both sides:
   \[
   n = 22
   \]

**Final Answer:**

The flower bed has a total of **22 rows**.

***********************<think>
First, I need to determine the common difference of the arithmetic progression.

Given that there are 100 terms in total and the last term is 212, which corresponds to \(a_{100} = 212\). The sixth term is given as 24, so \(a_6 = 24\).

Using the formula for the nth term of an arithmetic progression:
\(a_n = a + (n-1)d\), where 'a' is the first term and 'd' is the common difference.

For the sixth term:  
24 = a + 5d ...(1)

For the last term, which is the 100th term:  
212 = a + 99d ...(2)

Subtracting equation (1) from equation (2):  
(212 - 24) = (a + 99d) - (a + 5d)  
188 = 94d  
So, d = 2.

Now that I have the common difference, substitute it back into equation (1) to find 'a':  
24 = a + 5*2  
24 = a + 10  
Thus, a = 14.

With both 'a' and 'd' known, I can find the 58th term:  
\(a_{58} = 14 + (58 - 1)*2 = 14 + 57*2 = 14 + 114 = 128\).
</think>

To solve for the **58th** term of an Arithmetic Progression (AP) given that there are **100 terms**, the **6th term is 24**, and the **last term is 212**, follow these steps:

### Step 1: Understand the Formulas

In an AP:
- The \(n^{th}\) term (\(a_n\)) is given by:
  \[
  a_n = a + (n - 1)d
  \]
  where:
    - \(a\) = first term
    - \(d\) = common difference
    - \(n\) = number of terms

### Step 2: Set Up Equations Based on Given Information

1. **6th Term (\(a_6\)) is 24**:
   \[
   a + (6 - 1)d = 24 \\
   a + 5d = 24 \quad \text{(Equation 1)}
   \]
   
2. **Last Term (\(a_{100}\)) is 212**:
   \[
   a + (100 - 1)d = 212 \\
   a + 99d = 212 \quad \text{(Equation 2)}
   \]

### Step 3: Solve for Common Difference (\(d\))

Subtract Equation 1 from Equation 2 to eliminate \(a\):
\[
(a + 99d) - (a + 5d) = 212 - 24 \\
94d = 188 \\
d = \frac{188}{94} \\
d = 2
\]

### Step 4: Solve for First Term (\(a\)) Using \(d = 2\) in Equation 1

\[
a + 5(2) = 24 \\
a + 10 = 24 \\
a = 24 - 10 \\
a = 14
\]

### Step 5: Find the **58th** Term (\(a_{58}\))

Using the formula for \(n^{th}\) term:
\[
a_{58} = a + (58 - 1)d \\
a_{58} = 14 + 57 \times 2 \\
a_{58} = 14 + 114 \\
a_{58} = 128
\]

### Final Answer

The **58th** term of the Arithmetic Progression is:

First, I remember that in an arithmetic progression (AP), each term after the first is obtained by adding a constant difference. Looking at the terms: 24 to 21 is a decrease of 3, and 21 to 18 is also a decrease of 3. So, it's clear that this is an AP with the first term being 24 and the common difference (d) is -3.

The user wants to know how many terms (let's call this number n) are needed so that their sum equals 78. I recall the formula for the sum of the first n terms of an AP: 

S_n = n/2 * [2a + (n - 1)d]

Where:
- S_n is the sum of the first n terms,
- a is the first term,
- d is the common difference.

S_n = 78
a = 24
d = -3

78 = (n/2) * [2*24 + (n - 1)*(-3)]

n^2 - 17n + 52 = 0

Now we have a quadratic equation: n^2 - 17n + 52 = 0.

n = [17 ± sqrt( (-17)^2 - 4*1*52 )]/(2*1)

Calculating inside the square root:
(-17)^2 = 289
4*1*52 = 208

Thus, n = [17 ± 9]/2

This gives two solutions:
n = (17 + 9)/2 = 26/2 = 13
n = (17 - 9)/2 = 8/2 = 4

So, the possible values for n are 4 and 13. 

Wait a second, we need to check if both these solutions make sense in context. Since n represents the number of terms, it can't be negative or a fraction.

Term 2: 21
Term 3: 18
Term 4: 15

Sum = 24 + 21 + 18 + 15 = 78. That works.

= (13/2)*(48 - 36)
= (13/2)*12
= 13*6
= 78

But practically, when would we need to take 13 terms? It's possible mathematically, but maybe not contextually relevant here. However, since both values satisfy the equation, both are technically correct answers.

Therefore, there are two possible numbers of terms: 4 and 13.

To find the common difference, I'll use the formula for the nth term of an arithmetic progression.

The 34th term is \(a_{34} = a + (34 - 1)d\).

Similarly, the 20th term is \(a_{20} = a + (20 - 1)d\).

To determine the common difference of the Arithmetic Progression (AP), let's follow these steps:

### Step 1: Understand the Formula for the nth Term of an AP
The general formula for the \(n^{th}\) term of an AP is:
\[ a_n = a + (n - 1)d \]
where:
- \(a\) is the first term,
- \(d\) is the common difference,
- \(n\) is the term number.

### Step 2: Express the Given Terms Using the Formula
Given:
- The 34th term (\(a_{34}\)):
\[ a_{34} = a + (34 - 1)d \]
\[ a_{34} = a + 33d \]

- The 20th term (\(a_{20}\)):
\[ a_{20} = a + (20 - 1)d \]
\[ a_{20} = a + 19d \]

### Step 3: Set Up the Equation Based on the Given Information
We know that:
\[ a_{34} - a_{20} = 14 \]

Substituting the expressions from above:
\[ (a + 33d) - (a + 19d) = 14 \]
\[ a + 33d - a - 19d = 14 \]
\[ 14d = 14 \]

### Step 4: Solve for the Common Difference (\(d\))
\[ d = \frac{14}{14} \]
\[ d = 1 \]

### Final Answer
The common difference of the AP is:
\[
\boxed{1}
\]

**Solution:**

We need to calculate the **Geometric Mean** of the given annual percentage growth rates of profits from 2000 to 2005. The formula for the geometric mean is:

\[
\text{Geometric Mean} = \left( \prod_{i=1}^{n} x_i \right)^{\frac{1}{n}}
\]

**Given Data:**
- **Values:** \(50, 72, 54, 82, 93\)
- **Number of values (\(n\)):** \(5\)

**Step-by-Step Calculation:**

1. **Multiply all the given values:**
   \[
   50 \times 72 \times 54 \times 82 \times 93 = 15,895,680
   \]

2. **Take the fifth root of the product (since there are 5 numbers):**
   \[
   \sqrt[5]{15,895,680} \approx 71.41
   \]

**Final Answer:**

\[
\boxed{\text{Final Answer} : 71.41}
\]

To determine the value of \( k \), we'll use the concept of **Arithmetic Mean**.

### Given:
- Numbers: 9, 8, 10, \( k \), 12
- Arithmetic Mean (\( AM \)) = 15

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Understand the Formula for Arithmetic Mean:**
   
   The arithmetic mean of a set of numbers is given by:
   
   \[
   AM = \frac{\text{Sum of all numbers}}{\text{Total number of numbers}}
   \]
   
2. **Set Up the Equation:**
   
   Given that there are 5 numbers in total (including \( k \)), we can write:
   
   \[
   15 = \frac{9 + 8 + 10 + k + 12}{5}
   \]
   
3. **Calculate the Sum of Known Numbers:**
   
   Add up the known values:
   
   \[
   9 + 8 + 10 + 12 = 39
   \]
   
4. **Substitute Back into the Equation:**
   
   Plugging this sum back in:
   
   \[
   15 = \frac{39 + k}{5}
   \]
   
5. **Solve for \( k \):**
   
   Multiply both sides by 5 to eliminate the denominator:
   
   \[
   75 = 39 + k
   \]
   
   Subtract 39 from both sides:
   
   \[
   k = 75 - 39
   \]
   
   \[
   k = 36
   \]

### Final Answer:

\[
\boxed{36}
\]

\]

\]

\]

\[

First, I need to understand the problem. The arithmetic mean of 40 numbers is initially calculated as 38 based on a misinterpreted value.

I recognize that the incorrect sum was used to find this mean. Therefore, I calculate the incorrect total sum by multiplying the mean by the number of values: 40 * 38 = 1520.

Next, it's discovered that one number was incorrectly recorded as 36 instead of its true value, 56. This means the incorrect sum is short by (56 - 36) = 20 points compared to the actual values.

To correct the sum, I add these missing 20 points back to the initial incorrect total: 1520 + 20 = 1540.

**Solution**

Let's break down the problem step by step to find the correct arithmetic mean.

### Step 1: Understand the Given Information
- **Incorrect Mean**: The initially calculated arithmetic mean of 40 numbers is **38**.
  
  \[
  \text{Incorrect Mean} = 38
  \]
  
- **Misinterpreted Value**: One number was incorrectly recorded as **36** instead of its actual value, which is **56**.

### Step 2: Calculate the Incorrect Total Sum
The arithmetic mean is calculated by dividing the total sum of all values by the number of values. Therefore, we can find the incorrect total sum (\(S_{\text{incorrect}}\)) as follows:

\[
\text{Incorrect Mean} = \frac{S_{\text{incorrect}}}{n}
\]
  
Substituting the known values:
  
\[
38 = \frac{S_{\text{incorrect}}}{40}
\]

Multiply both sides by 40 to find \(S_{\text{incorrect}}\):

\[
S_{\text{incorrect}} = 38 \times 40 = 1520
\]

### Step 3: Correct the Total Sum Due to Misinterpretation
The number was misinterpreted as **36** instead of **56**, which means it was undercounted by:

\[
56 - 36 = 20 \text{ points}
\]

To find the correct total sum (\(S_{\text{correct}}\)), add these missing points back to \(S_{\text{incorrect}}\):

\[
S_{\text{correct}} = S_{\text{incorrect}} + 20 = 1520 + 20 = 1540
\]

### Step 4: Calculate the Correct Arithmetic Mean
Now, calculate the correct arithmetic mean (\(\bar{X}_{\text{correct}}\)) using the corrected total sum:

\[
\bar{X}_{\text{correct}} = \frac{S_{\text{correct}}}{n} = \frac{1540}{40}
\]

Performing the division:

\[
\bar{X}_{\text{correct}} = 38.5
\]

### Final Answer

```json
{'Final Answer': '38.5'}
```
